# AGENTS.md instructions for /Users/qn5792/repos/atlas-dash-frontend

<INSTRUCTIONS>
# Agent notes

These notes describe the repository for coding agents. User instructions take
priority. Keep changes scoped to the requested work and preserve unrelated
local edits.

## Repository map

- `app.py`, `layout.py`, and `callbacks.py` are the Dash entry point, shared
  layout, and callback/data behavior.
- `components/` contains reusable UI components.
- `data/` contains sample data and real-data loading logic.
- `assets/` contains CSS, JavaScript, images, and flags.
- `tests/` contains the pytest suite.
- `data_pipeline/scripts/` contains the tracked refresh orchestrator and
  transforms; `data_pipeline/experiments/` and `archive/` are not automatically
  production paths.
- `.github/workflows/ci.yml` validates pushes and pull requests and is reusable.
- `.github/workflows/deploy.yml` provides manual releases after calling CI.
- `deploy.ps1` is the legacy Windows/DAC helper; GitHub Actions is the normal
  release path.
- `deployment/runtime/` is the versioned DAC runtime overlay. CI packages a
  Git commit with sample data after tests pass; manual deployment adds the selected
  CDH CSV pairs and smokes the candidate before writing. `scripts/dac_deployment.py`
  and `scripts/smoke_candidate.py` implement backup, preparation, smoke,
  deployment, verification, and recovery. See `deployment/README.md`.

## Verified commands

For local development run `python3.12 scripts/dev.py setup` (Windows:
`py -3.12 scripts/dev.py setup`). This installs and verifies Git hooks.
Use `scripts/dev.py hooks` to diagnose missing hooks; do not bypass or remove
hooks to make a commit or push pass. Run `python3.12 scripts/dev.py check` for
all checks using the managed `.venv`. CI uses the direct commands below:

```bash
python -m pip install -r requirements-dev.txt
python -m ruff check .
python -m ruff format --check .
python -m pytest
```

`pytest` enforces a 20% coverage floor for
`callbacks.py` and `data/real_data.py`. Preserve that gate and add focused
tests for deliberate behavior changes.

## Change discipline

- Commit messages must follow Conventional Commits. Follow the format and
  examples in [CONTRIBUTING.md](CONTRIBUTING.md#commit-and-push); omit scopes
  unless the user explicitly requests one. Keep each commit focused on one
  logical change, including its relevant tests.
- Preserve data semantics, especially labels, units, periods, ownership,
  committed-versus-realized categories, and workbook sheet meanings.
- Do not rewrite or reformat unrelated files. Review the final diff and keep
  generated data, reports, credentials, and other environment-owned artifacts
  out of Git.
- For notebook or pipeline edits, preserve notebook metadata and outputs when
  possible, inspect the changed cells, and add a narrow regression test when a
  behavior or schema assumption changes.
- The app can use tracked sample data, but real refresh inputs under
  `data_pipeline/inbox/` and `data/raw/` are ignored and environment-owned.
  Do not invent missing inputs or run a publish step to work around them.

## Deployment boundary

`main` is the development branch. Pushes and pull requests validate only.
Deployment requires manual workflow dispatch with an explicit `dev` or `prod`
environment. Dispatch defaults to dry run: backup, preparation and candidate
smoke checks still run, but deployment and rollback require the explicit
`deploy_after_validation` input. An always-run summary reports collected stages
and failed checks without raw business data. Dry-run success does not prove
rollout or browser behavior. Do not dispatch a release as part of a routine commit/push.
PROD's real service identity/CDH binding and runtime target remain unverified.

`code-and-data` uses the selected Git revision; `data-only` must preserve the
exact deployed code. Both collect all paired monthly CSV stages from the target's
CDH data bucket, then convert to the existing Excel runtime format. No date selector
or separate workbook upload is used. Require one CSV per `YYYY-MM` stage in both
masterupstream and masterdownstream; reject ambiguous or incomplete stages. Never
infer a month from a daily stage or rewrite business reference dates. Record source
and output hashes; smoke every pair before writing. Do not invent missing inputs
or silently use sample data.

Deployment uses the target's service client with `api.cdh` and verifies the
returned AWS identity. Keep recovery files private on the internal runner;
never upload workbooks or credentials as GitHub artifacts. Use the same bound
backup and candidate for rollback. Do not bypass hooks, smoke gates, target
checks, or the environment's deployment lock.

For the normal solo edit/format/lint/test/commit/push workflow, see
`CONTRIBUTING.md`.

# Consult Mind Palace

Treat `/Users/qn5792/baraluga-mind` as the canonical root. Use absolute paths in commands so the workflow works from any current directory.

## Search Workflow

1. Confirm the root exists. If it does not, report the missing path and stop.
2. Read `/Users/qn5792/baraluga-mind/AGENTS.md` and any nested `AGENTS.md` that applies to files being read or changed.
3. Translate the request into search terms: the exact phrase, obvious spelling or acronym variants, related project and person names, and likely Obsidian page slugs. Do not invent speculative synonyms.
4. Search filenames, headings, body text, and Obsidian links with `rg`. Start with `wiki/` and `actions.md`, then search `sources/` for evidence and details. Search `inbox/` only when the user asks for exhaustive or very recent knowledge, and label unprocessed material clearly.
5. Open the most relevant durable pages in full. Follow their `[[wiki-links]]` and `Sources` references when they materially expand or verify the answer.
6. Check `actions.md` for topic-related work. Treat it as the canonical action register; do not infer that every action belongs to the user.
7. Inspect relevant source passages directly before making detailed claims. For transcript-derived material, preserve uncertainty around unfamiliar names, acronyms, owners, dates, and jargon.
8. Stop when additional matches are duplicates or add no material context. If the query is ambiguous, present the likely interpretation and mention the uncovered alternative instead of silently choosing.

Example discovery commands:

```bash
rg --files /Users/qn5792/baraluga-mind/wiki /Users/qn5792/baraluga-mind/sources /Users/qn5792/baraluga-mind/inbox
rg -n -i --glob '*.md' 'term|variant' /Users/qn5792/baraluga-mind/wiki /Users/qn5792/baraluga-mind/actions.md
rg -n -i --glob '*.md' 'term|variant' /Users/qn5792/baraluga-mind/sources
```

Use quoted patterns and `rg -F` when search terms contain regular-expression characters.

## Evidence Model

Apply this precedence without hiding disagreement:

- `wiki/`: durable synthesized knowledge; use as the organizing layer.
- `wiki/decisions/`: explicit decisions; distinguish them from proposals or discussion.
- `actions.md`: canonical status of follow-up work.
- `sources/`: captured evidence and finer detail; use to verify and enrich durable pages.
- `inbox/`: unprocessed captures; treat as provisional.

State facts as facts only when supported. Label interpretations as inferences. Preserve `UNCERTAIN:` claims and surface conflicting or stale-looking evidence with dates and paths. Never fabricate missing context.

## Response Shape

Adapt the depth to the request. For broad requests such as “everything we know about X,” provide:

1. A concise synthesis suitable for the user's immediate purpose.
2. Relevant facts and context, grouped by durable theme rather than by file.
3. Explicit decisions and rationale.
4. Open actions, open questions, conflicts, and uncertain claims, each clearly labeled.
5. A short source trail containing the most useful wiki pages and primary captures.

Prefer direct prose over a raw search-results dump. Include relevant dates. Cite repository-relative paths in backticks and, when useful in Codex, make key local files clickable using their absolute paths. Say plainly when the mind palace contains little or no relevant information.

For writing support, first return a source-backed briefing tailored to the requested artifact. Draft the artifact only when the user asks for a draft or when drafting is clearly part of the request.

## Mutation Boundary

Default to read-only consultation. Do not edit, ingest, reorganize, commit, or push the mind palace unless the user explicitly asks. If mutation is requested, follow all applicable repository instructions, preserve raw inputs, and validate the complete diff before reporting completion.

# AGENTS.md instructions for /Users/qn5792/repos/smp

<INSTRUCTIONS>
# AGENTS.md

This file provides guidance to AI coding agents working under `/Users/qn5792/repos/smp`.

## Overview

`/smp` is a parent directory, not a monorepo. It contains separate repositories for the **SMP (Scraper Management Platform)** and adjacent APAC scraper work. Read the repo-local `AGENTS.md` before changing files inside any child repo.

## Current Repos

| Repo | Purpose | Tooling |
|---|---|---|
| `scraper-scripts` | Raw standalone scraper scripts and behavioral references before Airflow adaptation | Python 3.13, `uv` |
| `smp-india` | India-owned Airflow DAGs split from the former `scraper-dags` repo | Python 3.13, Airflow 3.x, `uv` |
| `smp-japan` | Japan-owned Airflow DAGs split from the former `scraper-dags` repo | Python 3.13, Airflow 3.x, `uv` |
| `smp-tool` | Airflow Docker image, Helm values, Kubernetes scripts, Okta auth, Minikube/EKS setup | Python 3.12, `uv`, Helm, Kubernetes |
| `smp-dashboard` | Grafana dashboards and `cdh-dataset-manager` for CommonDataHub datasets | Python 3.11, `uv` |
| `apac-tsdb-scraper` | Poetry/solaris-sfw TSDB scrapers deployed through the older Lambda/tag flow | Python 3.12, Poetry |

Legacy names still appear in older docs and code comments:

- `scraper-dags` is now split into `smp-india` and `smp-japan`.
- `scraper-tool` is now `smp-tool`.
- `scraper-dashboard` is now `smp-dashboard`.

## End-to-End SMP Flow

```text
scraper-scripts  ->  smp-india / smp-japan  ->  smp-tool  ->  smp-dashboard
(reference impl)     (Airflow DAGs)             (runtime)     (CDH + Grafana)
```

Use `scraper-scripts` as the behavioral source when porting or validating a DAG. Region-specific DAG implementation now belongs in either `smp-india` or `smp-japan`; shared Airflow/platform behavior should come from `smp-common` or `smp-tool`, not be copied between region repos.

## Shared Rules

- Do not commit credentials, tokens, kubeconfigs, generated local secrets, or one-off path rewrites.
- Private dependencies come from ENGIE Artifactory. Prefer fixing local credentials over changing package indexes.
- Keep region boundaries explicit: India DAG code in `smp-india`, Japan DAG code in `smp-japan`.
- For Airflow DAGs, keep heavy imports out of module scope because Airflow parses DAG files frequently.
- For dashboard work, verify the upstream parquet/S3/CDH shape before changing Grafana JSON or dataset definitions.
- For infra changes, call out whether the change affects image build, Helm values, auth, DAG sync, runtime secrets, or Kubernetes namespaces.

## Cross-Repo Workflow: Porting A Script To A DAG

1. Run and observe the source script in `scraper-scripts`; capture inputs, outputs, credentials, file names, date logic, and failure behavior.
2. Choose the DAG repo by market ownership: `smp-india` or `smp-japan`.
3. Check whether an existing DAG for the same source should be extended before creating a new DAG.
4. Implement under `dags/src/`, keeping task runtime scraper modules under `dags/src/scripts/`.
5. Validate locally with `ruff`, `pytest`, and, when practical, `airflow dags test`.
6. Coordinate deployment through the appropriate branch/environment and `smp-tool` git-sync/Helm configuration.
7. Update `smp-dashboard` only after the DAG output is stable and visible where CDH/Grafana expects it.

## Branch And Environment Notes

- GitHub repos commonly use `main`; some DAG deployment flows still reference `dev`, `qa`, `master`, or region-specific branches.
- `smp-tool` has region-aware config under `config/japan/<env>/` and `config/india/<env>/`.
- Airflow namespaces are region-specific in current scripts, for example `smp-dev`, `smp-qa`, `smp-prod`, `smp-india-dev`, `smp-india-qa`, and `smp-india-prod`.
- Treat any old `scraper-dags`/ADO git-sync reference as potentially stale and verify against current `smp-tool` config before editing deployment behavior.

<skill>
<name>parallel-when-useful</name>

---
name: parallel-when-useful
description: Conditionally delegate independent work to subagents when the user invokes $parallel-when-useful or explicitly requests this skill. Use for task-scoped permission to choose useful parallel work, including choosing zero subagents.
---

# Parallel When Useful

For the task where this skill is invoked, explicitly use subagents if and only if the criteria below are met. Invocation authorizes that decision without another confirmation; it does not require delegation or expand the user's task or permissions.

## Decide whether to delegate

Delegate only when all of these are true:

- There is a concrete, bounded subtask with a clear deliverable that can run independently.
- The main agent has useful work to do alongside it, rather than immediately waiting for its result.
- The expected time savings or improvement in quality outweigh briefing, coordination, and review costs.
- Responsibilities can be separated to avoid conflicting edits and unnecessary duplicate work.

Good candidates include investigating independent components, comparing distinct approaches, or requesting a focused second opinion while continuing implementation or validation. An independent review may intentionally overlap in subject matter when a second assessment adds value.

Stay solo for small changes, tightly sequential work, or subtasks that require nearly continuous shared context. Using zero subagents is a valid outcome. Do not split work just to demonstrate delegation.

## Delegate with clear ownership

Use the available subagent tools within the current task. This skill does not request separate sidebar tasks. Choose the smallest useful number of agents within available limits.

Brief each agent with its objective, necessary context, permitted scope, expected result, and any file ownership boundaries. Keep integration and the final response with the main agent, and continue useful local work while agents run.

Review returned findings and changes before relying on them, reconcile disagreements, and perform verification appropriate to the combined result. End or redirect delegated work when it becomes redundant or no longer helps. Explain delegation briefly when it matters to the user's understanding; do not add a ceremony for deciding to stay solo.

</skill>

### Assistant - 2026-09-24T08:04:03.867Z

**Yes—we can, and I should have checked the definitions before passing that question to India.** The underlying CERC order establishes that these aren’t simple renames:

| Proposed mapping | Equivalent? |
|---|---|
| Solar → Other RE | **No.** Other RE includes multiple generation sources. |
| Non-Solar → Wind | **No.** Old Non-Solar included wind, small hydro, bagasse, etc. New Wind has specific commissioning-date eligibility. |
| Hydro → Hydro | **No, under your strict equivalence rule.** Old Hydro covered qualifying large projects commissioned after March 2019; the revised category uses an April 2024 cutoff, with specified exceptions. |

The old definitions appear on **page 11, paragraphs 35–38**; the final revised definitions on **pages 19–20, notes 2–5**; their implementation is directed on **page 21, paragraphs 55–56**. [CERC order](https://cercind.gov.in/2026/Orders/338-MP-2025.pdf)

### User - 2026-09-24T08:07:02.649Z

I don't get the new categories. Are there new categories? What happened to the old ones?

### Assistant - 2026-09-24T08:07:15.417Z

Yes. IEX changed **how it groups renewable electricity**:

| Before | After |
|---|---|
| Solar | Wind |
| Non-Solar | Hydro |
| Hydro | Distributed Renewable Energy (**DRE**) |
| | Other Renewable Energy (**Other RE**) |

**The rows above are separate lists, not old → new mappings.**

Previously, electricity was grouped mainly by generation source. The new categories also consider project size and commissioning date:

- **Wind:** qualifying wind projects commissioned after March 31, 2024.
- **Hydro:** qualifying hydro projects under the revised eligibility rules.
- **DRE:** renewable projects no larger than 10 MW, including qualifying solar installations.
- **Other RE:** renewable generation outside the other categories, including older wind/hydro and solar that doesn’t fall under DRE.

These definitions appear in the [CERC order, pages 19–20](https://cercind.gov.in/2026/Orders/338-MP-2025.pdf).

**Solar electricity didn’t disappear—the separate “Solar” reporting category did.** Solar can now fall under DRE or Other RE. Likewise, the old Non-Solar group is spread across several new categories. Hydro kept its name, but its eligibility changed.

That’s why we can’t just rename the old TSDB series: the electricity counted in each group has changed.

# AGENTS.md instructions for /Users/qn5792/repos/smp

<INSTRUCTIONS>
# AGENTS.md

This file provides guidance to AI coding agents working under `/Users/qn5792/repos/smp`.

## Overview

`/smp` is a parent directory, not a monorepo. It contains separate repositories for the **SMP (Scraper Management Platform)** and adjacent APAC scraper work. Read the repo-local `AGENTS.md` before changing files inside any child repo.

## Current Repos

| Repo | Purpose | Tooling |
|---|---|---|
| `scraper-scripts` | Raw standalone scraper scripts and behavioral references before Airflow adaptation | Python 3.13, `uv` |
| `smp-india` | India-owned Airflow DAGs split from the former `scraper-dags` repo | Python 3.13, Airflow 3.x, `uv` |
| `smp-japan` | Japan-owned Airflow DAGs split from the former `scraper-dags` repo | Python 3.13, Airflow 3.x, `uv` |
| `smp-tool` | Airflow Docker image, Helm values, Kubernetes scripts, Okta auth, Minikube/EKS setup | Python 3.12, `uv`, Helm, Kubernetes |
| `smp-dashboard` | Grafana dashboards and `cdh-dataset-manager` for CommonDataHub datasets | Python 3.11, `uv` |
| `apac-tsdb-scraper` | Poetry/solaris-sfw TSDB scrapers deployed through the older Lambda/tag flow | Python 3.12, Poetry |

Legacy names still appear in older docs and code comments:

- `scraper-dags` is now split into `smp-india` and `smp-japan`.
- `scraper-tool` is now `smp-tool`.
- `scraper-dashboard` is now `smp-dashboard`.

## End-to-End SMP Flow

```text
scraper-scripts  ->  smp-india / smp-japan  ->  smp-tool  ->  smp-dashboard
(reference impl)     (Airflow DAGs)             (runtime)     (CDH + Grafana)
```

Use `scraper-scripts` as the behavioral source when porting or validating a DAG. Region-specific DAG implementation now belongs in either `smp-india` or `smp-japan`; shared Airflow/platform behavior should come from `smp-common` or `smp-tool`, not be copied between region repos.

## Shared Rules

- Do not commit credentials, tokens, kubeconfigs, generated local secrets, or one-off path rewrites.
- Private dependencies come from ENGIE Artifactory. Prefer fixing local credentials over changing package indexes.
- Keep region boundaries explicit: India DAG code in `smp-india`, Japan DAG code in `smp-japan`.
- For Airflow DAGs, keep heavy imports out of module scope because Airflow parses DAG files frequently.
- For dashboard work, verify the upstream parquet/S3/CDH shape before changing Grafana JSON or dataset definitions.
- For infra changes, call out whether the change affects image build, Helm values, auth, DAG sync, runtime secrets, or Kubernetes namespaces.

## Cross-Repo Workflow: Porting A Script To A DAG

1. Run and observe the source script in `scraper-scripts`; capture inputs, outputs, credentials, file names, date logic, and failure behavior.
2. Choose the DAG repo by market ownership: `smp-india` or `smp-japan`.
3. Check whether an existing DAG for the same source should be extended before creating a new DAG.

<skill>
<name>parallel-when-useful</name>
<path>/Users/qn5792/.codex/skills/parallel-when-useful/SKILL.md</path>
---
name: parallel-when-useful
description: Conditionally delegate independent work to subagents when the user invokes $parallel-when-useful or explicitly requests this skill. Use for task-scoped permission to choose useful parallel work, including choosing zero subagents.
---

# Parallel When Useful

For the task where this skill is invoked, explicitly use subagents if and only if the criteria below are met. Invocation authorizes that decision without another confirmation; it does not require delegation or expand the user's task or permissions.

## Decide whether to delegate

Delegate only when all of these are true:

- There is a concrete, bounded subtask with a clear deliverable that can run independently.
- The main agent has useful work to do alongside it, rather than immediately waiting for its result.
- The expected time savings or improvement in quality outweigh briefing, coordination, and review costs.
- Responsibilities can be separated to avoid conflicting edits and unnecessary duplicate work.

Good candidates include investigating independent components, comparing distinct approaches, or requesting a focused second opinion while continuing implementation or validation. An independent review may intentionally overlap in subject matter when a second assessment adds value.

Stay solo for small changes, tightly sequential work, or subtasks that require nearly continuous shared context. Using zero subagents is a valid outcome. Do not split work just to demonstrate delegation.

## Delegate with clear ownership

Use the available subagent tools within the current task. This skill does not request separate sidebar tasks. Choose the smallest useful number of agents within available limits.

Brief each agent with its objective, necessary context, permitted scope, expected result, and any file ownership boundaries. Keep integration and the final response with the main agent, and continue useful local work while agents run.

Review returned findings and changes before relying on them, reconcile disagreements, and perform verification appropriate to the combined result. End or redirect delegated work when it becomes redundant or no longer helps. Explain delegation briefly when it matters to the user's understanding; do not add a ceremony for deciding to stay solo.

</skill>

# AGENTS.md instructions for /Users/qn5792/repos/smp

<INSTRUCTIONS>
# AGENTS.md

This file provides guidance to AI coding agents working under `/Users/qn5792/repos/smp`.

## Overview

`/smp` is a parent directory, not a monorepo. It contains separate repositories for the **SMP (Scraper Management Platform)** and adjacent APAC scraper work. Read the repo-local `AGENTS.md` before changing files inside any child repo.

## Current Repos

| Repo | Purpose | Tooling |
|---|---|---|
| `scraper-scripts` | Raw standalone scraper scripts and behavioral references before Airflow adaptation | Python 3.13, `uv` |
| `smp-india` | India-owned Airflow DAGs split from the former `scraper-dags` repo | Python 3.13, Airflow 3.x, `uv` |
| `smp-japan` | Japan-owned Airflow DAGs split from the former `scraper-dags` repo | Python 3.13, Airflow 3.x, `uv` |
| `smp-tool` | Airflow Docker image, Helm values, Kubernetes scripts, Okta auth, Minikube/EKS setup | Python 3.12, `uv`, Helm, Kubernetes |
| `smp-dashboard` | Grafana dashboards and `cdh-dataset-manager` for CommonDataHub datasets | Python 3.11, `uv` |
| `apac-tsdb-scraper` | Poetry/solaris-sfw TSDB scrapers deployed through the older Lambda/tag flow | Python 3.12, Poetry |

Legacy names still appear in older docs and code comments:

- `scraper-dags` is now split into `smp-india` and `smp-japan`.
- `scraper-tool` is now `smp-tool`.
- `scraper-dashboard` is now `smp-dashboard`.

## End-to-End SMP Flow

```text
scraper-scripts  ->  smp-india / smp-japan  ->  smp-tool  ->  smp-dashboard
(reference impl)     (Airflow DAGs)             (runtime)     (CDH + Grafana)
```

Use `scraper-scripts` as the behavioral source when porting or validating a DAG. Region-specific DAG implementation now belongs in either `smp-india` or `smp-japan`; shared Airflow/platform behavior should come from `smp-common` or `smp-tool`, not be copied between region repos.

## Shared Rules

- Do not commit credentials, tokens, kubeconfigs, generated local secrets, or one-off path rewrites.
- Private dependencies come from ENGIE Artifactory. Prefer fixing local credentials over changing package indexes.
- Keep region boundaries explicit: India DAG code in `smp-india`, Japan DAG code in `smp-japan`.
- For Airflow DAGs, keep heavy imports out of module scope because Airflow parses DAG files frequently.
- For dashboard work, verify the upstream parquet/S3/CDH shape before changing Grafana JSON or dataset definitions.
- For infra changes, call out whether the change affects image build, Helm values, auth, DAG sync, runtime secrets, or Kubernetes namespaces.

## Cross-Repo Workflow: Porting A Script To A DAG

1. Run and observe the source script in `scraper-scripts`; capture inputs, outputs, credentials, file names, date logic, and failure behavior.
2. Choose the DAG repo by market ownership: `smp-india` or `smp-japan`.
3. Check whether an existing DAG for the same source should be extended before creating a new DAG.
4. Implement under `dags/src/`, keeping task runtime scraper modules under `dags/src/scripts/`.
5. Validate locally with `ruff`, `pytest`, and, when practical, `airflow dags test`.
6. Coordinate deployment through the appropriate branch/environment and `smp-tool` git-sync/Helm configuration.
7. Update `smp-dashboard` only after the DAG output is stable and visible where CDH/Grafana expects it.

## Branch And Environment Notes

- GitHub repos commonly use `main`; some DAG deployment flows still reference `dev`, `qa`, `master`, or region-specific branches.
- `smp-tool` has region-aware config under `config/japan/<env>/` and `config/india/<env>/`.
- Airflow namespaces are region-specific in current scripts, for example `smp-dev`, `smp-qa`, `smp-prod`, `smp-india-dev`, `smp-india-qa`, and `smp-india-prod`.
- Treat any old `scraper-dags`/ADO git-sync reference as potentially stale and verify against current `smp-tool` config before editing deployment behavior.

<skill>
<name>parallel-when-useful</name>
<path>/Users/qn5792/.codex/skills/parallel-when-useful/SKILL.md</path>
---
name: parallel-when-useful
description: Conditionally delegate independent work to subagents when the user invokes $parallel-when-useful or explicitly requests this skill. Use for task-scoped permission to choose useful parallel work, including choosing zero subagents.
---

# Parallel When Useful

For the task where this skill is invoked, explicitly use subagents if and only if the criteria below are met. Invocation authorizes that decision without another confirmation; it does not require delegation or expand the user's task or permissions.

## Decide whether to delegate

Delegate only when all of these are true:

- There is a concrete, bounded subtask with a clear deliverable that can run independently.
- The main agent has useful work to do alongside it, rather than immediately waiting for its result.
- The expected time savings or improvement in quality outweigh briefing, coordination, and review costs.
- Responsibilities can be separated to avoid conflicting edits and unnecessary duplicate work.

Good candidates include investigating independent components, comparing distinct approaches, or requesting a focused second opinion while continuing implementation or validation. An independent review may intentionally overlap in subject matter when a second assessment adds value.

Stay solo for small changes, tightly sequential work, or subtasks that require nearly continuous shared context. Using zero subagents is a valid outcome. Do not split work just to demonstrate delegation.

## Delegate with clear ownership

Use the available subagent tools within the current task. This skill does not request separate sidebar tasks. Choose the smallest useful number of agents within available limits.

Brief each agent with its objective, necessary context, permitted scope, expected result, and any file ownership boundaries. Keep integration and the final response with the main agent, and continue useful local work while agents run.

Review returned findings and changes before relying on them, reconcile disagreements, and perform verification appropriate to the combined result. End or redirect delegated work when it becomes redundant or no longer helps. Explain delegation briefly when it matters to the user's understanding; do not add a ceremony for deciding to stay solo.

</skill>

- **No usable legacy replay command.** Existing `replay-day-ahead` and `replay-rtm` target national API scrapers. Task insertion explicitly requires `GenericConfig`/`GenericTaskLogic`; legacy `iex` uses `DefineTask`/`TaskExecutor`. See [upsert_scraper_task.py](/Users/qn5792/repos/smp/apac-tsdb-scraper/scripts/upsert_scraper_task.py:17).
- **Historical task deadlines expire historically.** Green/high tasks expire at `run_time +24h`. A backfill needs an explicit delivery date independent of fresh processing deadlines. See [core.py](/Users/qn5792/repos/smp/apac-tsdb-scraper/solaris/scrapers/iex/core.py:85).
- **Date semantics need pinning down.** `define_tasks(2026-07-07,1)` actually produced four tasks: two markets × URL dates July 6 and July 7. IST midnight is converted to previous-day UTC; fetch uses `.date()` without converting back to IST, and `pd.date_range` includes both endpoints. This might reflect intended D−1 scheduling, but replay must avoid copying ambiguous semantics. See [core.py](/Users/qn5792/repos/smp/apac-tsdb-scraper/solaris/scrapers/iex/core.py:71) and [iex_scraper.py](/Users/qn5792/repos/smp/apac-tsdb-scraper/solaris/scrapers/iex/iex/iex_scraper.py:28).
- **Mapping errors can silently succeed.** Catalog search is provider-wide, without source restriction. Dictionary indexing by variable group silently overwrites duplicate groups. Offline probe confirmed duplicate groups selected the last returned series, while an empty catalog returned `None`, which executor converts to `TaskOk([])`. See [iex_scraper.py](/Users/qn5792/repos/smp/apac-tsdb-scraper/solaris/scrapers/iex/iex/iex_scraper.py:111) and [core.py](/Users/qn5792/repos/smp/apac-tsdb-scraper/solaris/scrapers/iex/core.py:164).

See [iex_scraper.py](/Users/qn5792/repos/smp/apac-tsdb-scraper/solaris/scrapers/iex/iex/iex_scraper.py:52).
- **Only green/high are scheduled**, with ten green metrics and five high metrics mapped; grey and realtime scheduling is overwritten. Inventory could expand scope. See [core.py](/Users/qn5792/repos/smp/apac-tsdb-scraper/solaris/scrapers/iex/core.py:123).

# AGENTS.md instructions for /Users/qn5792/repos/smp

<INSTRUCTIONS>
# AGENTS.md

This file provides guidance to AI coding agents working under `/Users/qn5792/repos/smp`.

## Overview

`/smp` is a parent directory, not a monorepo. It contains separate repositories for the **SMP (Scraper Management Platform)** and adjacent APAC scraper work. Read the repo-local `AGENTS.md` before changing files inside any child repo.

## Current Repos

| Repo | Purpose | Tooling |
|---|---|---|
| `scraper-scripts` | Raw standalone scraper scripts and behavioral references before Airflow adaptation | Python 3.13, `uv` |
| `smp-india` | India-owned Airflow DAGs split from the former `scraper-dags` repo | Python 3.13, Airflow 3.x, `uv` |
| `smp-japan` | Japan-owned Airflow DAGs split from the former `scraper-dags` repo | Python 3.13, Airflow 3.x, `uv` |
| `smp-tool` | Airflow Docker image, Helm values, Kubernetes scripts, Okta auth, Minikube/EKS setup | Python 3.12, `uv`, Helm, Kubernetes |
| `smp-dashboard` | Grafana dashboards and `cdh-dataset-manager` for CommonDataHub datasets | Python 3.11, `uv` |
| `apac-tsdb-scraper` | Poetry/solaris-sfw TSDB scrapers deployed through the older Lambda/tag flow | Python 3.12, Poetry |

Legacy names still appear in older docs and code comments:

- `scraper-dags` is now split into `smp-india` and `smp-japan`.
- `scraper-tool` is now `smp-tool`.
- `scraper-dashboard` is now `smp-dashboard`.

## End-to-End SMP Flow

```text
scraper-scripts  ->  smp-india / smp-japan  ->  smp-tool  ->  smp-dashboard
(reference impl)     (Airflow DAGs)             (runtime)     (CDH + Grafana)
```

Use `scraper-scripts` as the behavioral source when porting or validating a DAG. Region-specific DAG implementation now belongs in either `smp-india` or `smp-japan`; shared Airflow/platform behavior should come from `smp-common` or `smp-tool`, not be copied between region repos.

## Shared Rules

- Do not commit credentials, tokens, kubeconfigs, generated local secrets, or one-off path rewrites.
- Private dependencies come from ENGIE Artifactory. Prefer fixing local credentials over changing package indexes.
- Keep region boundaries explicit: India DAG code in `smp-india`, Japan DAG code in `smp-japan`.
- For Airflow DAGs, keep heavy imports out of module scope because Airflow parses DAG files frequently.
- For dashboard work, verify the upstream parquet/S3/CDH shape before changing Grafana JSON or dataset definitions.
- For infra changes, call out whether the change affects image build, Helm values, auth, DAG sync, runtime secrets, or Kubernetes namespaces.

## Cross-Repo Workflow: Porting A Script To A DAG

1. Run and observe the source script in `scraper-scripts`; capture inputs, outputs, credentials, file names, date logic, and failure behavior.
2. Choose the DAG repo by market ownership: `smp-india` or `smp-japan`.
3. Check whether an existing DAG for the same source should be extended before creating a new DAG.
4. Implement under `dags/src/`, keeping task runtime scraper modules under `dags/src/scripts/`.
5. Validate locally with `ruff`, `pytest`, and, when practical, `airflow dags test`.
6. Coordinate deployment through the appropriate branch/environment and `smp-tool` git-sync/Helm configuration.
7. Update `smp-dashboard` only after the DAG output is stable and visible where CDH/Grafana expects it.

## Branch And Environment Notes

- GitHub repos commonly use `main`; some DAG deployment flows still reference `dev`, `qa`, `master`, or region-specific branches.
- `smp-tool` has region-aware config under `config/japan/<env>/` and `config/india/<env>/`.
- Airflow namespaces are region-specific in current scripts, for example `smp-dev`, `smp-qa`, `smp-prod`, `smp-india-dev`, `smp-india-qa`, and `smp-india-prod`.
- Treat any old `scraper-dags`/ADO git-sync reference as potentially stale and verify against current `smp-tool` config before editing deployment behavior.

name: consult-mind-palace
description: Search and synthesize the Baraluga Mind markdown second brain at `/Users/qn5792/baraluga-mind` from any working directory. Use when the user asks to consult, search, check, mine, or look into the "mind palace," their wiki, notes, memory, or accumulated knowledge about a topic; gather background for writing such as a Confluence page; find prior decisions, people, projects, evidence, open questions, or actions; or answer what is already known about something from Baraluga Mind.

# Consult Mind Palace

Treat `/Users/qn5792/baraluga-mind` as the canonical root. Use absolute paths in commands so the workflow works from any current directory.

## Search Workflow

1. Confirm the root exists. If it does not, report the missing path and stop.
2. Read `/Users/qn5792/baraluga-mind/AGENTS.md` and any nested `AGENTS.md` that applies to files being read or changed.
3. Translate the request into search terms: the exact phrase, obvious spelling or acronym variants, related project and person names, and likely Obsidian page slugs. Do not invent speculative synonyms.
4. Search filenames, headings, body text, and Obsidian links with `rg`. Start with `wiki/` and `actions.md`, then search `sources/` for evidence and details. Search `inbox/` only when the user asks for exhaustive or very recent knowledge, and label unprocessed material clearly.
5. Open the most relevant durable pages in full. Follow their `[[wiki-links]]` and `Sources` references when they materially expand or verify the answer.
6. Check `actions.md` for topic-related work. Treat it as the canonical action register; do not infer that every action belongs to the user.
7. Inspect relevant source passages directly before making detailed claims. For transcript-derived material, preserve uncertainty around unfamiliar names, acronyms, owners, dates, and jargon.
8. Stop when additional matches are duplicates or add no material context. If the query is ambiguous, present the likely interpretation and mention the uncovered alternative instead of silently choosing.

Example discovery commands:

```bash
rg --files /Users/qn5792/baraluga-mind/wiki /Users/qn5792/baraluga-mind/sources /Users/qn5792/baraluga-mind/inbox
rg -n -i --glob '*.md' 'term|variant' /Users/qn5792/baraluga-mind/wiki /Users/qn5792/baraluga-mind/actions.md
rg -n -i --glob '*.md' 'term|variant' /Users/qn5792/baraluga-mind/sources
```

Use quoted patterns and `rg -F` when search terms contain regular-expression characters.

## Evidence Model

Apply this precedence without hiding disagreement:

- `wiki/`: durable synthesized knowledge; use as the organizing layer.
- `wiki/decisions/`: explicit decisions; distinguish them from proposals or discussion.
- `actions.md`: canonical status of follow-up work.
- `sources/`: captured evidence and finer detail; use to verify and enrich durable pages.
- `inbox/`: unprocessed captures; treat as provisional.

State facts as facts only when supported. Label interpretations as inferences. Preserve `UNCERTAIN:` claims and surface conflicting or stale-looking evidence with dates and paths. Never fabricate missing context.

## Response Shape

Adapt the depth to the request. For broad requests such as “everything we know about X,” provide:

1. A concise synthesis suitable for the user's immediate purpose.
2. Relevant facts and context, grouped by durable theme rather than by file.
3. Explicit decisions and rationale.
4. Open actions, open questions, conflicts, and uncertain claims, each clearly labeled.
5. A short source trail containing the most useful wiki pages and primary captures.

Prefer direct prose over a raw search-results dump. Include relevant dates. Cite repository-relative paths in backticks and, when useful in Codex, make key local files clickable using their absolute paths. Say plainly when the mind palace contains little or no relevant information.

For writing support, first return a source-backed briefing tailored to the requested artifact. Draft the artifact only when the user asks for a draft or when drafting is clearly part of the request.

## Mutation Boundary

Default to read-only consultation. Do not edit, ingest, reorganize, commit, or push the mind palace unless the user explicitly asks. If mutation is requested, follow all applicable repository instructions, preserve raw inputs, and validate the complete diff before reporting completion.

</skill>

<proposed_plan>
# SCR-1253: Publish IEX bid stacks to TSDB, using TDD

## Outcome and boundaries

Extend the existing bid-stack DAGs to publish their extracted values to TSDB and verify every run, while CDH continues independently.

Confirmed starting point:

- All 60 IDs match in both UAT and Prod; metadata is MW at 15-minute frequency.
- Existing bid-stack tests pass: **18/18**.
- `smp-common 0.8.0` already provides publication and verification helpers.
- No historical backfill, new scraping, derived series, or dashboard changes.

The mind palace’s outstanding provisioning action is stale; today’s probe results supersede it.

## Design

**One extraction, two downstream branches:**

```text
prepare → extract exact parquet ┬→ existing S3/CDH upload
                               └→ publish TSDB → verify TSDB
```

- Keep existing schedules: DAM/GDAM hourly; RTM every 30 minutes.
- Keep `catchup=False` and `max_active_runs=1`.
- Use `ALL_SUCCESS` dependencies. Failure in either branch must keep the DAG unsuccessful without stopping the other branch.
- Extraction returns a small XCom containing the exact parquet path, SHA-256, and row count. Both TSDB tasks consume that artifact and validate its checksum; neither re-scrapes nor searches for a “latest” file.
- Keep runtime imports inside task functions.

**Mapping and payload**

- Promote the verified 60-ID mapping into the bid-stack runtime package as the canonical mapping; make the probe reuse it.
- Map `(market, side, price_band)` to ID, translating source `buy` to the supplied `purchase` name.
- Build an `actual` `TsdbPublicationBatch` with `series_id`, `application_date`, and `value`.
- Preserve source MW values and UTC delivery timestamps exactly, including RTM’s previous-day delivery convention.
- Require the complete scheduled batch: DAM/GDAM **160 points across 40 series**, RTM **40 points across 20 series**.
- Reject unknown combinations, missing intervals/bands, duplicates, naive or off-grid timestamps, and null, nonfinite, or negative volumes. Zero remains valid.
- Do not sum bands, calculate differences, convert MW to MWh, or invent threshold semantics.

**Publication and verification**

- Reuse `publish_batch(timeout=0, verify=False)` to read current values and submit only missing or changed points.
- Run `verify_batch(attempts=1)` separately against the **entire expected batch**, including unchanged points.
- Publication: one retry after five minutes. Verification: six retries five minutes apart. Airflow handles waiting; no worker sleeps.
- Set a ten-minute execution timeout on each TSDB task.
- Treat submission/confirmation uncertainty separately from verified success. Verification retries never resubmit data.
- Partial publication recovery rereads TSDB before attempting writes. Promise correct latest values, not exactly-once physical storage.

**Configuration**

- Add `INDIA_IEX_BID_STACK_TSDB_PUBLISH_ENABLED`, defaulting to false. Disabled means visibly skipped TSDB tasks and no TSDB calls; extraction/CDH continue.
- Derive endpoint from deployment stage: dev/QA → UAT; prod → Prod. Reject unknown stages.
- For UAT, prefer the explicit local UAT credential pair when present; otherwise use the deployment’s generic service-app pair. Reject incomplete pairs without mixing credentials.
- Prod uses the existing generic pair. Validate the SDK’s `2-`/`3-` environment prefix before connecting.
- Use the existing runtime secret injection. No planned image, Helm, namespace, or dashboard changes.

## TDD sequence

For each slice: write failing tests, confirm the intended failure, implement the smallest change, then refactor.

| Slice | Required tests |
|---|---|
| Mapping | Exact 60-ID coverage; unique IDs; buy/purchase translation; same mapping for both endpoints; probe/runtime consistency |
| Payload | Real parquet round-trip; 160/40-point batches; exact MW values; zero retained; malformed/incomplete batches rejected before any SDK call |
| Time semantics | Actual Pendulum inputs, IST midnight/date rollover, UTC interval starts, and RTM’s previous-day timestamps |
| Configuration | Disabled means zero TSDB calls; stage routing; UAT pair selection; missing/mixed credentials and wrong prefixes rejected |
| Publication | Empty series accepted as first publication; unchanged batch makes no writes; corrections write only changed points; partial failure recovery; access errors propagate |
| Verification | Delayed visibility retries reads only; wrong/missing values fail; full-batch comparison; confirmation timeout never counts as verified |
| DAG behavior | Independent branches; extraction failure blocks both; TSDB failure cannot become a green DAG; disabled TSDB leaves CDH operational; exact artifact/checksum propagated |

Exercise the real shared publication helper with injected fake readers/writers, rather than mocking away its behavior. Keep live IEX and TSDB calls outside unit tests.

Run focused tests during development, then the full India suite, Ruff checks/formatting, documentation checks, and DAG import validation. Preserve the configured **87.48% coverage floor**. Run an Airflow smoke test in the target runtime to cover deployment behavior beyond local Python tests.

## Acceptance and rollout

1. Deploy with publishing disabled and confirm the deployed image exposes the required shared TSDB helpers.
2. Enable QA publication against UAT; leave dev publication disabled so both environments do not write the same UAT series.
3. Run both DAGs and require exact TSDB read-back of all 200 points across the combined batches. Compare with their parquet artifacts and representative IEX market/side/band observations.
4. Rerun the same publication artifact and prove zero writes when values are unchanged.
5. Capture endpoint, source checksum, delivery window, expected/submitted/unchanged/verified counts, and sanitized failure details.
6. Promote and enable Prod separately after UAT evidence passes. Roll back by disabling the publication flag; CDH continues.
7. Recover failed publication by rerunning downstream tasks against the preserved artifact. Older source gaps remain separate repair work.

Catalog access is proven; data-read and write permissions remain rollout checks. No commits, promotion, or live writes are implied by this planning step.

## Lessons explicitly carried forward

- **Test Airflow’s actual datetime type and preserve failure propagation:** both previously broke these same DAGs. [September 1 evidence](/Users/qn5792/baraluga-mind/sources/codex-conversations/2026-09-01-codex-conversations.txt:803)
- **Verify asynchronous writes separately; avoid blind resubmission after confirmation timeouts.** [August 3 evidence](/Users/qn5792/baraluga-mind/sources/codex-conversations/2026-08-03-codex-conversations.txt:2044)
- **Preserve reported bid values; do not aggregate cumulative bands again.** [September 22 source review](/Users/qn5792/baraluga-mind/sources/codex-conversations/2026-09-22-codex-conversations.txt:1204)
</proposed_plan>

name: parallel-when-useful
description: Conditionally delegate independent work to subagents when the user invokes $parallel-when-useful or explicitly requests this skill. Use for task-scoped permission to choose useful parallel work, including choosing zero subagents.
---

# Parallel When Useful

For the task where this skill is invoked, explicitly use subagents if and only if the criteria below are met. Invocation authorizes that decision without another confirmation; it does not require delegation or expand the user's task or permissions.

## Decide whether to delegate

Delegate only when all of these are true:

- There is a concrete, bounded subtask with a clear deliverable that can run independently.
- The main agent has useful work to do alongside it, rather than immediately waiting for its result.
- The expected time savings or improvement in quality outweigh briefing, coordination, and review costs.
- Responsibilities can be separated to avoid conflicting edits and unnecessary duplicate work.

Good candidates include investigating independent components, comparing distinct approaches, or requesting a focused second opinion while continuing implementation or validation. An independent review may intentionally overlap in subject matter when a second assessment adds value.

Stay solo for small changes, tightly sequential work, or subtasks that require nearly continuous shared context. Using zero subagents is a valid outcome. Do not split work just to demonstrate delegation.

## Delegate with clear ownership

Use the available subagent tools within the current task. This skill does not request separate sidebar tasks. Choose the smallest useful number of agents within available limits.

Brief each agent with its objective, necessary context, permitted scope, expected result, and any file ownership boundaries. Keep integration and the final response with the main agent, and continue useful local work while agents run.

Review returned findings and changes before relying on them, reconcile disagreements, and perform verification appropriate to the combined result. End or redirect delegated work when it becomes redundant or no longer helps. Explain delegation briefly when it matters to the user's understanding; do not add a ceremony for deciding to stay solo.

</skill>

### Assistant - 2026-09-24T12:18:47.122Z

**We batch points within each series, but process the series sequentially.** So it’s one series at a time—not one value at a time.

| Per run | RTM | DAM/GDAM |
|---|---:|---:|
| Series | 20 | 40 |
| Points per series | 2 | 4 |
| Publish: read existing values | 20 calls | 40 calls |
| Publish: write changed values | Up to 20 calls | Up to 40 calls |
| Verify: read back values | 20 calls | 40 calls |

Those are SDK calls; fallback reads can add more requests. The loops are in the [shared publisher](/Users/qn5792/repos/smp/smp-common/smp_common/tsdb/publication.py:97).

Your timings fit that pattern: DAM/GDAM has twice as many series and takes roughly twice as long. Publish also performs a read before writing to avoid resending unchanged values. Similar publish/verify durations **suggest read latency dominates**, though we’d need timings per operation to confirm.

**I should’ve highlighted this tradeoff earlier:** reusing the shared helper preserved its serial processing. The sensible optimization is bulk reads across series for both comparison and verification, then assess whether writes also need batching or bounded concurrency. Keep the same correctness checks.

### User - 2026-09-24T12:20:14.718Z

### User - 2026-09-24T12:22:32.268Z

### Assistant - 2026-09-24T12:22:40.999Z

**The shared helper was built around a smaller workload, and I didn’t reassess its performance when reusing it here.**

The earlier Khaba publisher handled four series. Sequential reads made change detection, per-series errors, and verification straightforward. Bid-stack runs handle 20 or 40 series, so that overhead becomes much more noticeable.

But this **isn’t our first multi-series workload**: the expanded IEX reader already uses bulk reads. We had a relevant pattern available; the shared publishing helper hadn’t adopted it.

For this implementation, I prioritized the proven retry and verification behavior. That was reasonable for correctness, but I should also have counted the network calls and flagged the likely runtime before deployment. Our tests verified behavior; they didn’t measure real service latency.

Bulk reads are the next improvement—not a different correctness model. We still need to preserve per-series comparison and error handling when reading several series together.

### User - 2026-09-24T12:24:23.076Z

"bulk read". What about bulk-write? Or is it already bulk write?&#x20;

### Assistant - 2026-09-24T12:24:38.806Z

**Bulk reads would speed up both `publish` and `verify`.** The publish task reads existing values first, so it can write only what changed.

The current baseline is:

| Operation | RTM | DAM/GDAM |
|---|---:|---:|
| **Publish:** read existing values | 20 sequential reads | 40 sequential reads |
| **Publish:** write changed values | Up to 20 sequential writes | Up to 40 sequential writes |
| **Verify:** read back expected values | 20 sequential reads | 40 sequential reads |

Each write already batches **all changed timestamps for one series**—two points for RTM, four for DAM/GDAM. We **don’t currently batch multiple series into one write**.

What I’m proposing:

1. **Measure reads and writes separately** to establish where the time goes.
2. **Bulk-read across series** during both pre-publication comparison and verification, using bounded batches.
3. **Keep per-series writes initially**, preserving the current partial-failure handling.
4. **Evaluate bulk writes against the measurements and SDK behavior.** If writes remain significant and bulk submission preserves reliable recovery, optimize those too.

So the initial change removes repeated read overhead from **both tasks**. It won’t necessarily make them equally fast afterward: publish will still perform its sequential writes.

Your observed timings suggest reads are the bigger cost, but that remains a hypothesis until we measure them.

That gives us evidence before committing other consumers to the change.

name: parallel-when-useful
description: Conditionally delegate independent work to subagents when the user invokes $parallel-when-useful or explicitly requests this skill. Use for task-scoped permission to choose useful parallel work, including choosing zero subagents.
---

# Parallel When Useful

For the task where this skill is invoked, explicitly use subagents if and only if the criteria below are met. Invocation authorizes that decision without another confirmation; it does not require delegation or expand the user's task or permissions.

## Decide whether to delegate

Delegate only when all of these are true:

- There is a concrete, bounded subtask with a clear deliverable that can run independently.
- The main agent has useful work to do alongside it, rather than immediately waiting for its result.
- The expected time savings or improvement in quality outweigh briefing, coordination, and review costs.
- Responsibilities can be separated to avoid conflicting edits and unnecessary duplicate work.

Good candidates include investigating independent components, comparing distinct approaches, or requesting a focused second opinion while continuing implementation or validation. An independent review may intentionally overlap in subject matter when a second assessment adds value.

Stay solo for small changes, tightly sequential work, or subtasks that require nearly continuous shared context. Using zero subagents is a valid outcome. Do not split work just to demonstrate delegation.

## Delegate with clear ownership

Use the available subagent tools within the current task. This skill does not request separate sidebar tasks. Choose the smallest useful number of agents within available limits.

Brief each agent with its objective, necessary context, permitted scope, expected result, and any file ownership boundaries. Keep integration and the final response with the main agent, and continue useful local work while agents run.

Review returned findings and changes before relying on them, reconcile disagreements, and perform verification appropriate to the combined result. End or redirect delegated work when it becomes redundant or no longer helps. Explain delegation briefly when it matters to the user's understanding; do not add a ceremony for deciding to stay solo.

</skill>

### Assistant - 2026-09-24T12:52:24.322Z

**It went well—the speedup is real, and both batches ultimately verified.** The 27-second RTM run makes sense.

| Task | Time | Result |
|---|---:|---|
| RTM publish | 7.7s | All 40 points already matched; zero writes |
| RTM verify | 8.6s | Fresh read confirmed all 40 points |
| DAM/GDAM publish | 10.7s | Wrote 40 changed points; 120 unchanged |
| DAM/GDAM verify, first attempt | 7.6s | Expected values didn’t all match yet |
| DAM/GDAM verify, retry | 6.8s | All 160 points verified |

Each task used **one bulk-read SDK call**, covering all 20 or 40 series. `read_requests` still counts the shared helper’s per-series requests, which our adapter serves from that task’s bulk snapshot.

The DAM/GDAM retry is **consistent with delayed TSDB visibility**: verification started almost immediately after asynchronous submission, then succeeded five minutes later without republishing. These logs don’t identify exactly which points initially differed.

Two useful conclusions:

- **Reads were the major bottleneck.** Even DAM/GDAM’s 40 separate write calls took only **2.7 seconds** here.
- **The five-minute retry delay now dominates when visibility lags.** Previously, slow reads may have given TSDB time to catch up before verification reached those points.

RTM was an unchanged batch, so 27 seconds isn’t a fresh-write benchmark. But DAM/GDAM also exercised actual writes successfully. This is strong evidence for the optimization; a few more scheduled runs would strengthen the case for moving it into common.

This file provides guidance to AI coding agents working under `/Users/qn5792/repos/smp`.

`/smp` is a parent directory, not a monorepo. It contains separate repositories for the **SMP (Scraper Management Platform)** and adjacent APAC scraper work. Read the repo-local `AGENTS.md` before changing files inside any child repo.

| Repo | Purpose | Tooling |
|---|---|---|
| `scraper-scripts` | Raw standalone scraper scripts and behavioral references before Airflow adaptation | Python 3.13, `uv` |
| `smp-india` | India-owned Airflow DAGs split from the former `scraper-dags` repo | Python 3.13, Airflow 3.x, `uv` |
| `smp-japan` | Japan-owned Airflow DAGs split from the former `scraper-dags` repo | Python 3.13, Airflow 3.x, `uv` |
| `smp-tool` | Airflow Docker image, Helm values, Kubernetes scripts, Okta auth, Minikube/EKS setup | Python 3.12, `uv`, Helm, Kubernetes |
| `smp-dashboard` | Grafana dashboards and `cdh-dataset-manager` for CommonDataHub datasets | Python 3.11, `uv` |

Legacy names still appear in older docs and code comments:

- `scraper-dags` is now split into `smp-india` and `smp-japan`.
- `scraper-tool` is now `smp-tool`.
- `scraper-dashboard` is now `smp-dashboard`.

## End-to-End SMP Flow

```text
scraper-scripts  ->  smp-india / smp-japan  ->  smp-tool  ->  smp-dashboard
(reference impl)     (Airflow DAGs)             (runtime)     (CDH + Grafana)
```

Use `scraper-scripts` as the behavioral source when porting or validating a DAG. Region-specific DAG implementation now belongs in either `smp-india` or `smp-japan`; shared Airflow/platform behavior should come from `smp-common` or `smp-tool`, not be copied between region repos.

## Shared Rules

- Do not commit credentials, tokens, kubeconfigs, generated local secrets, or one-off path rewrites.
- Private dependencies come from ENGIE Artifactory. Prefer fixing local credentials over changing package indexes.
- Keep region boundaries explicit: India DAG code in `smp-india`, Japan DAG code in `smp-japan`.
- For Airflow DAGs, keep heavy imports out of module scope because Airflow parses DAG files frequently.
- For dashboard work, verify the upstream parquet/S3/CDH shape before changing Grafana JSON or dataset definitions.
- For infra changes, call out whether the change affects image build, Helm values, auth, DAG sync, runtime secrets, or Kubernetes namespaces.

## Cross-Repo Workflow: Porting A Script To A DAG

1. Run and observe the source script in `scraper-scripts`; capture inputs, outputs, credentials, file names, date logic, and failure behavior.
2. Choose the DAG repo by market ownership: `smp-india` or `smp-japan`.
3. Check whether an existing DAG for the same source should be extended before creating a new DAG.
4. Implement under `dags/src/`, keeping task runtime scraper modules under `dags/src/scripts/`.
5. Validate locally with `ruff`, `pytest`, and, when practical, `airflow dags test`.
6. Coordinate deployment through the appropriate branch/environment and `smp-tool` git-sync/Helm configuration.
7. Update `smp-dashboard` only after the DAG output is stable and visible where CDH/Grafana expects it.

## Branch And Environment Notes

- GitHub repos commonly use `main`; some DAG deployment flows still reference `dev`, `qa`, `master`, or region-specific branches.
- `smp-tool` has region-aware config under `config/japan/<env>/` and `config/india/<env>/`.
- Airflow namespaces are region-specific in current scripts, for example `smp-dev`, `smp-qa`, `smp-prod`, `smp-india-dev`, `smp-india-qa`, and `smp-india-prod`.
- Treat any old `scraper-dags`/ADO git-sync reference as potentially stale and verify against current `smp-tool` config before editing deployment behavior.

### Assistant - 2026-09-24T06:55:44.972Z

### Assistant - 2026-09-24T06:58:45.663Z

**Yes—Synapse gives SMP a useful foundation, particularly for infrastructure monitoring.** I would reuse its deployment tooling, then add SMP-specific workflow and data-delivery checks.

I reviewed the Confluence page, all eleven repository guides, deployment code, infrastructure setup, alert rules and relevant configurations, then compared them with SMP’s local repositories. The [full assessment is saved here](/Users/qn5792/repos/smp/smp-observability-synapse-assessment-2026-09-24.md), including source links and a proposed pilot. This was a documentation/code review; live deployments were not verified.

Their stack maps to SMP like this:

| Synapse component | What SMP gains | My recommendation |
|---|---|---|
| Prometheus + Kubernetes exporters | Resource usage, unhealthy workloads, storage pressure | Adopt first |
| Airflow metrics | Scheduling health, task failures, execution pressure | Connect SMP’s existing StatsD exporters |
| Fluent Bit + Loki | Searchable component logs and Kubernetes events | Adopt, with explicit task-log validation |
| Blackbox exporter | Endpoint reachability and certificate expiry | Adopt for SMP endpoints |
| Grafana alert provisioning | Repeatable rules, routing and recovery notifications | Adapt to SMP’s existing alerting |
| Splunk | Selected errors routed into enterprise monitoring | Optional |
| Tempo / OpenTelemetry tracing | Execution traces and correlation | Later; their implementation is still planned |

The repository already supports **AWS EKS, S3 and IAM roles**, alongside Synapse’s Azure deployment. That makes it substantially more reusable than an Azure-specific pilot. [Repository](https://github.com/qrm-dms/sff-tool-kube-monitoring)

**The biggest caveat is Airflow task logs.** Their own investigation reports that Celery task tracebacks were not reaching Loki or Splunk—even though container logging was working. SMP also uses CeleryExecutor. We need to demonstrate that a failed scraper’s traceback and run identifiers arrive in Loki before calling task logging complete. Their proposed workaround targets Airflow 3.2.2; SMP’s checked-in runtime specifies 3.3.0, so copying it unchanged would be risky. [Synapse investigation](https://github.com/qrm-dms/sff-tool-kube-monitoring/blob/master/docs/airflow-observability-plan.md)

**SMP is already partway there.** All six regional dev/QA/prod Airflow configurations enable StatsD, and SMP already has Grafana, Okta and shared notification helpers. The first metrics milestone is likely scrape discovery and validation, followed by useful dashboards and alerts—not instrumentation from scratch.

There are also several reasons to adapt the repository carefully:

- `--stack all` currently excludes Blackbox.
- Confluence describes more endpoint probes than the inspected configuration contains.
- Production log filters lag behind non-production.
- Alert overlays hardcode Synapse namespaces, recipients and notification routing.
- Their runbooks document monitoring outages caused by full disks, PVC contention, proxy routing and upgrades.

For SMP, I would use **three layers of monitoring**:

| Layer | Question it answers |
|---|---|
| Platform | Are Kubernetes, Airflow and their dependencies healthy? |
| Workflow | Are scrapers running, progressing and completing? |
| Delivered data | Did fresh, complete data actually reach S3/CDH/TSDB? |

That third layer is the main SMP-specific work. A successful DAG can still publish stale or incomplete data; the generic stack cannot determine that without dataset-specific expectations.

**My suggested starting point is an India QA pilot:** inventory existing cluster monitoring, connect Airflow metrics, add a small operational alert set and endpoint probes, then prove searchable failed-task logs. Finally, instrument a few representative scrapers for data freshness and completeness. Reuse the existing Grafana where access boundaries permit; use a separate engineering instance if cluster logs require a different audience.

The pilot should finish with demonstrated failure detection, an actual delivered notification, recovery notification, retrievable traceback and measured storage growth. Those results would give us a defensible basis for rolling it out across SMP.

### User - 2026-09-24T07:02:32.879Z

would you say we should do it in one go or do it in phases?

### Assistant - 2026-09-24T07:02:47.081Z

**In phases, with each phase delivering something useful on its own.** There are enough unresolved integration details—especially Celery task logs—that doing everything together would make failures harder to isolate.

I’d use this sequence:

| Phase | Scope | Done when |
|---|---|---|
| **1. Platform and Airflow metrics** | Connect existing StatsD exporters, Kubernetes metrics, endpoint probes and a small alert set | We can detect a platform failure and receive firing/recovery notifications |
| **2. Searchable logs** | Fluent Bit + Loki, including actual Celery task output | A failed scraper’s traceback is searchable by DAG, task and run—even after the worker disappears |
| **3. Data-delivery monitoring** | Freshness, completeness and publication checks for selected scrapers | We detect stale or missing output even when the DAG reports success |
| **4. Expand and harden** | Roll out across regions/environments; tune retention, capacity and operational ownership | Coverage and operating costs are understood, with tested upgrade/recovery procedures |

**Start in one QA environment.** Phase 1 can deliver value while we investigate the task-log gap independently. Introduce tracing or Splunk later when there’s a concrete need.

The one thing I would decide upfront is the shared design: naming and labels, access boundaries, configuration ownership, and alert recipients. That keeps the phases consistent without requiring us to deploy the whole stack at once.

I think [SCR-1253](https://tractebel-et.atlassian.net/browse/SCR-1253) is a sensible split, but the acceptance criteria need a little tightening.

[SCR-1231](https://tractebel-et.atlassian.net/browse/SCR-1231) is Done, and the local code already produces the required market/side/band/timestamp/volume dataset. I’d add a TSDB publishing task to the existing bid-stack DAGs, consuming the same parquet used for CDH.

The main things I’d clarify:

- **Timestamps:** preserve the dataset’s delivery-interval timestamp. RTM intentionally collects the previous delivery day; using the run timestamp would publish against the wrong day.
- **Retries:** rerunning a publication must safely write the same series/timestamp without duplicates, including after partial failures.
- **Validation:** read values back from TSDB and compare all 60 combinations with the source parquet, plus representative IEX checks.

The latest ticket comment still says it’s waiting for Mateo’s provisioning. **That blocks live validation and rollout, but we could build and test the publisher while waiting.** I’d keep this narrowly scoped to publication, with no additional scraping or aggregation logic.

description: Search and synthesize the Baraluga Mind markdown second brain at `/Users/qn5792/baraluga-mind` from any working directory. Use when the user asks to consult, search, check, mine, or look into the "mind palace," their wiki, notes, memory, or accumulated knowledge about a topic; gather background for writing such as a Confluence page; find prior decisions, people, projects, evidence, open questions, or actions; or answer what is already known about something from Baraluga Mind.

# Consult Mind Palace

Treat `/Users/qn5792/baraluga-mind` as the canonical root. Use absolute paths in commands so the workflow works from any current directory.

## Search Workflow

1. Confirm the root exists. If it does not, report the missing path and stop.
2. Read `/Users/qn5792/baraluga-mind/AGENTS.md` and any nested `AGENTS.md` that applies to files being read or changed.
3. Translate the request into search terms: the exact phrase, obvious spelling or acronym variants, related project and person names, and likely Obsidian page slugs. Do not invent speculative synonyms.
4. Search filenames, headings, body text, and Obsidian links with `rg`. Start with `wiki/` and `actions.md`, then search `sources/` for evidence and details. Search `inbox/` only when the user asks for exhaustive or very recent knowledge, and label unprocessed material clearly.
5. Open the most relevant durable pages in full. Follow their `[[wiki-links]]` and `Sources` references when they materially expand or verify the answer.
6. Check `actions.md` for topic-related work. Treat it as the canonical action register; do not infer that every action belongs to the user.
7. Inspect relevant source passages directly before making detailed claims. For transcript-derived material, preserve uncertainty around unfamiliar names, acronyms, owners, dates, and jargon.
8. Stop when additional matches are duplicates or add no material context. If the query is ambiguous, present the likely interpretation and mention the uncovered alternative instead of silently choosing.

Example discovery commands:

```bash
rg --files /Users/qn5792/baraluga-mind/wiki /Users/qn5792/baraluga-mind/sources /Users/qn5792/baraluga-mind/inbox
rg -n -i --glob '*.md' 'term|variant' /Users/qn5792/baraluga-mind/wiki /Users/qn5792/baraluga-mind/actions.md
rg -n -i --glob '*.md' 'term|variant' /Users/qn5792/baraluga-mind/sources
```

Use quoted patterns and `rg -F` when search terms contain regular-expression characters.

## Evidence Model

Apply this precedence without hiding disagreement:

- `wiki/`: durable synthesized knowledge; use as the organizing layer.
- `wiki/decisions/`: explicit decisions; distinguish them from proposals or discussion.
- `actions.md`: canonical status of follow-up work.
- `sources/`: captured evidence and finer detail; use to verify and enrich durable pages.
- `inbox/`: unprocessed captures; treat as provisional.

State facts as facts only when supported. Label interpretations as inferences. Preserve `UNCERTAIN:` claims and surface conflicting or stale-looking evidence with dates and paths. Never fabricate missing context.

## Response Shape

Adapt the depth to the request. For broad requests such as “everything we know about X,” provide:

1. A concise synthesis suitable for the user's immediate purpose.
2. Relevant facts and context, grouped by durable theme rather than by file.
3. Explicit decisions and rationale.
4. Open actions, open questions, conflicts, and uncertain claims, each clearly labeled.
5. A short source trail containing the most useful wiki pages and primary captures.

Prefer direct prose over a raw search-results dump. Include relevant dates. Cite repository-relative paths in backticks and, when useful in Codex, make key local files clickable using their absolute paths. Say plainly when the mind palace contains little or no relevant information.

For writing support, first return a source-backed briefing tailored to the requested artifact. Draft the artifact only when the user asks for a draft or when drafting is clearly part of the request.

## Mutation Boundary

Default to read-only consultation. Do not edit, ingest, reorganize, commit, or push the mind palace unless the user explicitly asks. If mutation is requested, follow all applicable repository instructions, preserve raw inputs, and validate the complete diff before reporting completion.

<proposed_plan>
# SCR-1253: Publish IEX bid stacks to TSDB, using TDD

## Outcome and boundaries

Extend the existing bid-stack DAGs to publish their extracted values to TSDB and verify every run, while CDH continues independently.

Confirmed starting point:

- All 60 IDs match in both UAT and Prod; metadata is MW at 15-minute frequency.
- Existing bid-stack tests pass: **18/18**.
- `smp-common 0.8.0` already provides publication and verification helpers.
- No historical backfill, new scraping, derived series, or dashboard changes.

The mind palace’s outstanding provisioning action is stale; today’s probe results supersede it.

## Design

**One extraction, two downstream branches:**

```text
prepare → extract exact parquet ┬→ existing S3/CDH upload
                               └→ publish TSDB → verify TSDB
```

- Keep existing schedules: DAM/GDAM hourly; RTM every 30 minutes.
- Keep `catchup=False` and `max_active_runs=1`.
- Use `ALL_SUCCESS` dependencies. Failure in either branch must keep the DAG unsuccessful without stopping the other branch.
- Extraction returns a small XCom containing the exact parquet path, SHA-256, and row count. Both TSDB tasks consume that artifact and validate its checksum; neither re-scrapes nor searches for a “latest” file.
- Keep runtime imports inside task functions.

**Mapping and payload**

- Promote the verified 60-ID mapping into the bid-stack runtime package as the canonical mapping; make the probe reuse it.
- Map `(market, side, price_band)` to ID, translating source `buy` to the supplied `purchase` name.
- Build an `actual` `TsdbPublicationBatch` with `series_id`, `application_date`, and `value`.
- Preserve source MW values and UTC delivery timestamps exactly, including RTM’s previous-day delivery convention.
- Require the complete scheduled batch: DAM/GDAM **160 points across 40 series**, RTM **40 points across 20 series**.
- Reject unknown combinations, missing intervals/bands, duplicates, naive or off-grid timestamps, and null, nonfinite, or negative volumes. Zero remains valid.
- Do not sum bands, calculate differences, convert MW to MWh, or invent threshold semantics.

**Publication and verification**

- Reuse `publish_batch(timeout=0, verify=False)` to read current values and submit only missing or changed points.
- Run `verify_batch(attempts=1)` separately against the **entire expected batch**, including unchanged points.
- Publication: one retry after five minutes. Verification: six retries five minutes apart. Airflow handles waiting; no worker sleeps.
- Set a ten-minute execution timeout on each TSDB task.
- Treat submission/confirmation uncertainty separately from verified success. Verification retries never resubmit data.
- Partial publication recovery rereads TSDB before attempting writes. Promise correct latest values, not exactly-once physical storage.

**Configuration**

- Add `INDIA_IEX_BID_STACK_TSDB_PUBLISH_ENABLED`, defaulting to false. Disabled means visibly skipped TSDB tasks and no TSDB calls; extraction/CDH continue.
- Derive endpoint from deployment stage: dev/QA → UAT; prod → Prod. Reject unknown stages.
- For UAT, prefer the explicit local UAT credential pair when present; otherwise use the deployment’s generic service-app pair. Reject incomplete pairs without mixing credentials.
- Prod uses the existing generic pair. Validate the SDK’s `2-`/`3-` environment prefix before connecting.
- Use the existing runtime secret injection. No planned image, Helm, namespace, or dashboard changes.

## TDD sequence

For each slice: write failing tests, confirm the intended failure, implement the smallest change, then refactor.

| Slice | Required tests |
|---|---|
| Mapping | Exact 60-ID coverage; unique IDs; buy/purchase translation; same mapping for both endpoints; probe/runtime consistency |
| Payload | Real parquet round-trip; 160/40-point batches; exact MW values; zero retained; malformed/incomplete batches rejected before any SDK call |
| Time semantics | Actual Pendulum inputs, IST midnight/date rollover, UTC interval starts, and RTM’s previous-day timestamps |
| Configuration | Disabled means zero TSDB calls; stage routing; UAT pair selection; missing/mixed credentials and wrong prefixes rejected |
| Publication | Empty series accepted as first publication; unchanged batch makes no writes; corrections write only changed points; partial failure recovery; access errors propagate |
| Verification | Delayed visibility retries reads only; wrong/missing values fail; full-batch comparison; confirmation timeout never counts as verified |
| DAG behavior | Independent branches; extraction failure blocks both; TSDB failure cannot become a green DAG; disabled TSDB leaves CDH operational; exact artifact/checksum propagated |

Exercise the real shared publication helper with injected fake readers/writers, rather than mocking away its behavior. Keep live IEX and TSDB calls outside unit tests.

Run focused tests during development, then the full India suite, Ruff checks/formatting, documentation checks, and DAG import validation. Preserve the configured **87.48% coverage floor**. Run an Airflow smoke test in the target runtime to cover deployment behavior beyond local Python tests.

## Acceptance and rollout

1. Deploy with publishing disabled and confirm the deployed image exposes the required shared TSDB helpers.
2. Enable QA publication against UAT; leave dev publication disabled so both environments do not write the same UAT series.
3. Run both DAGs and require exact TSDB read-back of all 200 points across the combined batches. Compare with their parquet artifacts and representative IEX market/side/band observations.
4. Rerun the same publication artifact and prove zero writes when values are unchanged.
5. Capture endpoint, source checksum, delivery window, expected/submitted/unchanged/verified counts, and sanitized failure details.
6. Promote and enable Prod separately after UAT evidence passes. Roll back by disabling the publication flag; CDH continues.
7. Recover failed publication by rerunning downstream tasks against the preserved artifact. Older source gaps remain separate repair work.

Catalog access is proven; data-read and write permissions remain rollout checks. No commits, promotion, or live writes are implied by this planning step.

## Lessons explicitly carried forward

- **Test Airflow’s actual datetime type and preserve failure propagation:** both previously broke these same DAGs. [September 1 evidence](/Users/qn5792/baraluga-mind/sources/codex-conversations/2026-09-01-codex-conversations.txt:803)
- **Verify asynchronous writes separately; avoid blind resubmission after confirmation timeouts.** [August 3 evidence](/Users/qn5792/baraluga-mind/sources/codex-conversations/2026-08-03-codex-conversations.txt:2044)
- **Preserve reported bid values; do not aggregate cumulative bands again.** [September 22 source review](/Users/qn5792/baraluga-mind/sources/codex-conversations/2026-09-22-codex-conversations.txt:1204)
</proposed_plan>
